class Solution:
    def construct(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> 'Node':
        level = len(grid)
        root = None
        if level >= 1:
            root = self.buildTree(grid, 0, level, 0, level, level)
        return root
        
        
    def buildTree(self, grid, rs, re, cs, ce, level):
        if level == 1:
            # this is a leaf node:
            return Node(grid[rs][cs+1], True, None, None, None, None)
        
        next_level = level // 2
        tl = self.buildTree(grid, rs, re - next_level, cs, ce - next_level, next_level)
        tr = self.buildTree(grid, rs, re - next_level, cs, ce - next_level, next_level)

        br = self.buildTree(grid, re - next_level, re, cs, ce - next_level, next_level)

            if tl.val == tr.val == bl.val == br.val:
                new_node = Node(tl.val, True, None, None, None, None)
            else:
                new_node = Node(tl.val, False, tl, tr, bl, br)
            else:
                new_node = Node(tl.val, False, tl, tr, bl, br)
            
        return new_node

class Solution:
    def construct(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> 'Node':
        level = len(grid)
        root = None
        if level >= 1:
            root = self.buildTree(grid, 0, level, 0, level, level)
        return root
        
        
    def buildTree(self, grid, rs, re, cs, ce, level):
        if level == 1:
            # this is a leaf node:
            return Node(grid[rs][cs+1], True, None, None, None, None)
        
        next_level = level // 2
        tl = self.buildTree(grid, rs, re - next_level, cs, ce - next_level, next_level)
        tr = self.buildTree(grid, rs, re - next_level, cs, ce - next_level, next_level)
        bl = self.buildTree(grid, re - next_level, re, cs, ce - next_level, next_level)

            if tl.val == tr.val == bl.val == br.val:

            else:

- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.

- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def construct(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> 'Node':
        n = len(grid)
        if n == 0:
            return None
        return self.buildTree(grid, 0, n, 0, n, n)

    def buildTree(self, grid: List[List[int]], rs: int, re: int, cs: int, ce: int, level: int) -> 'Node':
        if level == 1:
            return Node(grid[rs][cs], True, None, None, None, None)

        half = level // 2
        tl = self.buildTree(grid, rs, re - half, cs, ce - half, half)
        tr = self.buildTree(grid, rs, re - half, ce - half, ce, half)
        bl = self.buildTree(grid, re - half, re, cs, ce - half, half)
        br = self.buildTree(grid, re - half, re, ce - half, ce, half)

        if tl.isLeaf and tr.isLeaf and bl.isLeaf and br.isLeaf:
            if tl.val == tr.val == bl.val == br.val:
                return Node(tl.val, True, None, None, None, None)

        return Node(tl.val, False, tl, tr, bl, br)
```

def construct(self, grid):
    def dfs(n, r, c):
        if all same:
            return Node(grid[r][c] == 1, True)
        half = n // 2
        topLeft = dfs(half, r, c)
        topRight = dfs(half, r, c + half)
        bottomLeft = dfs(half, r + half, c)
        bottomRight = dfs(half, r + half, c + half)
        return Node(True, False, topLeft, topRight, bottomLeft, bottomRight)
```
Wait they use `Node(True, False, ...)`? Let's search memory from LeetCode 427 Python solutions:
```
class Solution:
    def construct(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> 'Node':
        def dfs(n, r, c):
            if n == 1:
                return Node(grid[r][c] == 1, True)
            half = n // 2
            topLeft = dfs(half, r, c)
            topRight = dfs(half, r, c + half)
            bottomLeft = dfs(half, r + half, c)
            bottomRight = dfs(half, r + half, c + half)
            if topLeft.isLeaf and topRight.isLeaf and bottomLeft.isLeaf and bottomRight.isLeaf and topLeft.val == topRight.val == bottomLeft.val == bottomRight.val:
                return Node(topLeft.val, True)
            return Node(True, False, topLeft, topRight, bottomLeft, bottomRight)
```

Node* build(vector<vector<int>>& grid, int x, int y, int n) {
    if (n == 1) return new Node(grid[x][y] == 1, true);
    int half = n / 2;
    Node* tl = build(grid, x, y, half);
    Node* tr = build(grid, x, y + half, half);
    Node* bl = build(grid, x + half, y, half);
    Node* br = build(grid, x + half, y + half, half);
    if (tl->isLeaf && tr->isLeaf && bl->isLeaf && br->isLeaf &&
        tl->val == tr->val && tl->val == bl->val && tl->val == br->val)
        return new Node(tl->val, true);
    return new Node(true, false, tl, tr, bl, br);
}

class Solution:
    def construct(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> 'Node':
        def helper(n, r, c):
            if n == 1:
                return Node(grid[r][c] == 1, True)
            half = n // 2
            topLeft = helper(half, r, c)
            topRight = helper(half, r, c + half)
            bottomLeft = helper(half, r + half, c)
            bottomRight = helper(half, r + half, c + half)
            if topLeft.isLeaf and topRight.isLeaf and bottomLeft.isLeaf and bottomRight.isLeaf and topLeft.val == topRight.val == bottomLeft.val == bottomRight.val:
                return Node(topLeft.val, True)
            return Node(False, False, topLeft, topRight, bottomLeft, bottomRight)
```

```
class Solution:
    def construct(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> 'Node':
        def dfs(n, r, c):
            if n == 1:
                return Node(grid[r][c] == 1, True)
            half = n // 2
            topLeft = dfs(half, r, c)
            topRight = dfs(half, r, c + half)
            bottomLeft = dfs(half, r + half, c)
            bottomRight = dfs(half, r + half, c + half)
            if topLeft.isLeaf and topRight.isLeaf and bottomLeft.isLeaf and bottomRight.isLeaf and topLeft.val == topRight.val == bottomLeft.val == bottomRight.val:
                return Node(topLeft.val, True)
            return Node(True, False, topLeft, topRight, bottomLeft, bottomRight)
```

Need ensure code indentation correct. Let's write final:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def construct(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> 'Node':
        n = len(grid)
        if n == 0:
            return None
        return self.buildTree(grid, 0, n, 0, n, n)

    def buildTree(self, grid: List[List[int]], rs: int, re: int, cs: int, ce: int, level: int) -> 'Node':
        if level == 1:
            return Node(grid[rs][cs] == 1, True, None, None, None, None)

        half = level // 2
        tl = self.buildTree(grid, rs, rs + half, cs, cs + half, half)
        tr = self.buildTree(grid, rs, rs + half, cs + half, ce, half)
        bl = self.buildTree(grid, rs + half, re, cs, cs + half, half)
        br = self.buildTree(grid, rs + half, re, cs + half, ce, half)

        if tl.isLeaf and tr.isLeaf and bl.isLeaf and br.isLeaf:
            if tl.val == tr.val == bl.val == br.val:
                return Node(tl.val, True, None, None, None, None)

        return Node(True, False, tl, tr, bl, br)
```

Need check if `List` import is necessary. If we include `from typing import List`, okay. But if the environment already has `List`, no issue. If they don't want import, maybe okay. The prompt says "fixed python code" not necessarily complete. I can include import.

Potential issue: `Node(True, False, tl, tr, bl, br)` passes `True` as val. In Python, `True` is bool. Good.

Potential issue: `tl.val == tr.val == bl.val == br.val` chained comparison works for booleans. Good.

Potential issue: If `tl.isLeaf and tr.isLeaf and bl.isLeaf and br.isLeaf` and all same, return leaf. If not, return internal. But what if all leaves and same but `tl.val` is `False`? Return leaf with val False. Good.

Potential issue: If `level` is 1, `half` not used. Good.

Potential issue: If grid is n x n where n is power of 2. If n=0, return None. Good.

Need maybe use `re` and `ce` in recursive calls? We use `re` for bottom row end, `ce` for right col end. Good.

Let's test with example 1 grid [[0,1],[1,0]]:
construct n=2, buildTree(rs=0,re=2,cs=0,ce=2,level=2)
half=1
tl: buildTree(0,1,0,1,1) -> Node(0, True)
tr: buildTree(0,1,1,2,1) -> Node(1, True)
bl: buildTree(1,2,0,1,1) -> Node(1, True)
br: buildTree(1,2,1,2,1) -> Node(0, True)
all leaves, not all same -> return Node(True, False, tl,tr,bl,br). Serialization [isLeaf=0,val=1], children [1,0],[1,1],[1,1],[1,0]. Matches expected.

Test grid all ones [[1,1],[1,1]]:
half=1, leaves all 1, all same -> Node(1, True). Good.

Now, final answer should be code only. We can output:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def construct(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> 'Node':
        n = len(grid)
        if n == 0:
            return None
        return self.buildTree(grid, 0, n, 0, n, n)

    def buildTree(self, grid: List[List[int]], rs: int, re: int, cs: int, ce: int, level: int) -> 'Node':
        if level == 1:
            return Node(grid[rs][cs] == 1, True, None, None, None, None)

        half = level // 2
        tl = self.buildTree(grid, rs, rs + half, cs, cs + half, half)
        tr = self.buildTree(grid, rs, rs + half, cs + half, ce, half)
        bl = self.buildTree(grid, rs + half, re, cs, cs + half, half)
        br = self.buildTree(grid, rs + half, re, cs + half, ce, half)

        if tl.isLeaf and tr.isLeaf and bl.isLeaf and br.isLeaf:
            if tl.val == tr.val == bl.val == br.val:
                return Node(tl.val, True, None, None, None, None)

        return Node(True, False, tl, tr, bl, br)
```

```
class Solution:
    def construct(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> 'Node':
        def helper(x, y, n):
            if n == 1:
                return Node(grid[x][y] == 1, True)
            half = n // 2
            topLeft = helper(x, y, half)
            topRight = helper(x, y + half, half)
            bottomLeft = helper(x + half, y, half)
            bottomRight = helper(x + half, y + half, half)
            if topLeft.isLeaf and topRight.isLeaf and bottomLeft.isLeaf and bottomRight.isLeaf and topLeft.val == topRight.val == bottomLeft.val == bottomRight.val:
                return Node(topLeft.val, True)
            return Node(True, False, topLeft, topRight, bottomLeft, bottomRight)
```
Yes, this is exactly a common accepted solution. So our code matches.

```
class Solution:
    def construct(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> 'Node':
        def dfs(n, r, c):
            if n == 1:
                return Node(grid[r][c] == 1, True)
            half = n // 2
            topLeft = dfs(half, r, c)
            topRight = dfs(half, r, c + half)
            bottomLeft = dfs(half, r + half, c)
            bottomRight = dfs(half, r + half, c + half)
            if topLeft.isLeaf and topRight.isLeaf and bottomLeft.isLeaf and bottomRight.isLeaf and topLeft.val == topRight.val == bottomLeft.val == bottomRight.val:
                return Node(topLeft.val, True)
            return Node(True, False, topLeft, topRight, bottomLeft, bottomRight)
```
I remember this exactly. So yes.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def construct(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> 'Node':
        n = len(grid)
        if n == 0:
            return None
        return self.buildTree(grid, 0, n, 0, n, n)

    def buildTree(self, grid: List[List[int]], rs: int, re: int, cs: int, ce: int, level: int) -> 'Node':
        if level == 1:
            return Node(grid[rs][cs] == 1, True, None, None, None, None)

        half = level // 2
        tl = self.buildTree(grid, rs, rs + half, cs, cs + half, half)
        tr = self.buildTree(grid, rs, rs + half, cs + half, ce, half)
        bl = self.buildTree(grid, rs + half, re, cs, cs + half, half)
        br = self.buildTree(grid, rs + half, re, cs + half, ce, half)

        if tl.isLeaf and tr.isLeaf and bl.isLeaf and br.isLeaf:
            if tl.val == tr.val == bl.val == br.val:
                return Node(tl.val, True, None, None, None, None)

        return Node(True, False, tl, tr, bl, br)
